#### 2024 American Community Survey

| **Racial Makeup of Illinois (2024)**[^cite_note-CensusACS2024-112]  White alone (60.2%) Black alone (13.2%) Native American alone (0.83%) Asian Alone (6.39%) Pacific Islander Alone (0.04%) Some other race alone (8.66%) Two or more races (10.7%) | **Racial/Ethnic Makeup of Illinois excluding Hispanics from racial categories (2024)**[^cite_note-CensusACS2024-112] *NH=Non-Hispanic*  White NH (57.1%) Black NH (12.9%) Native American NH (0.11%) Asian NH (6.34%) Pacific Islander NH (0.02%) Other NH (0.33%) Two or more races NH (3.78%) Hispanic Any Race (19.4%) | **Racial Makeup of Hispanics in Illinois (2024)**[^cite_note-CensusACS2024-112]  White alone (16.0%) Black alone (1.45%) Native American alone (3.69%) Asian Alone (0.26%) Pacific Islander Alone (0.06%) Other race alone (43.0%) Two or more races (35.6%) |
| --- | --- | --- |

According to 2024 U.S. Census Bureau estimates (with Hispanics allocated amongst the various racial groups), Illinois' population was 60.2% [White](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_American), 13.2% Black or [African American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_American), 0.8% [Native American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_Americans_in_the_United_States) or [Alaskan Native](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaskan_Native), 6.4% [Asian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_American), 0.1% [Pacific Islander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Islander), 8.7% Some Other Race, and 10.7% from [two or more races](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiracial_American).[^cite_note-CensusACS2024-112] The white population continues to remain the largest racial category in Illinois. Under this methodology, Hispanics are allocated amongst the various racial groups and primarily identify as Some Other Race (43.0%) or Multiracial (35.6%) with the remainder identifying as White (16.0%), Black (1.5%), American Indian and Alaskan Native (3.7%), Asian (0.3%), and Hawaiian and Pacific Islander (0.1%).[^cite_note-CensusACS2024-112] By ethnicity, 19.4% of the total population is [Hispanic-Latino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanic_and_Latino_Americans) (of any race) and 80.6% is Non-Hispanic (of any race). Illinois has the largest share of Hispanic residents in the [Midwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midwestern_United_States), with over 37% of Hispanics in the region living in Illinois.[^cite_note-auto5-113][^cite_note-114]&#x20;

#### Hispanic Americans



[Little Village](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Village,_Chicago) neighborhood in Chicago

If treated as a separate category, Hispanics are the largest minority group in Illinois, making up nearly a fifth of the population.[^cite_note-CensusACS2023-115] About 75% of the state's Hispanic population is [Mexican-American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_Americans), numbering 1,794,369 and making up 14.3% of the total population of the state.[^cite_note-auto6-116] Illinois is home to the largest population of Mexican descent in the US outside of the [Western United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_United_States). The second largest Hispanic group in Illinois are [Puerto Ricans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puerto_Ricans), numbering 211,676 and making up 9% of the Hispanic population and 1.7% of the total population. The state is also home to significant Central American and South American communities, including 44,373 Guatemalan-Americans, 41,047 Ecuadorian-Americans, and 42,101 Colombian-Americans.[^cite_note-117] Hispanics are the second fastest growing demographic in Illinois after Asians, growing by 15% between 2010 and 2020, while the overall population of the state declined.&#x20;

The vast majority of Hispanics in Illinois live in Chicago or the surrounding suburbs, with 786,464 Hispanics living in the city of Chicago itself, making up nearly a third of the city's population. Many large suburbs of Chicago, including [Aurora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aurora,_Illinois),[^cite_note-118] [Berwyn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berwyn,_Illinois),[^cite_note-119] [Cicero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cicero,_Illinois),[^cite_note-120] [Elgin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elgin,_Illinois),[^cite_note-121] and [Waukegan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waukegan,_Illinois)[^cite_note-122] have either a majority or plurality Hispanic population. The county with the highest share of Hispanic residents in the state, at 33.5% of the population, is [Kane County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kane_County,_Illinois), located on the western edge of the Chicago suburbs and including the cities of Elgin and Aurora.[^cite_note-123] Hispanics have the lowest per-capita income of any major ethnic or racial group in Illinois, at \$28,541.[^cite_note-\:8-124] However, the Hispanic poverty rate, at 14.1%, is lower than that of Black residents, who have a poverty rate of 23%.[^cite_note-\:3-125] Hispanic residents are the youngest demographic group in Illinois, with a median age of 30.7.[^cite_note-126]&#x20;

While those of Hispanic ethnicity are not distinguished between total and partial Hispanic origin, 2021 estimates show that almost 10% of the state's Hispanic population also reported a non-Spanish European ancestry, with this group making up 1.7% of the state's total population.[^cite_note-auto3-127]&#x20;

#### African-Americans

Black Americans form the second largest minority group in Illinois. In 2023, 1,922,259 Illinoisans identified as Black alone or in combination, making up 15.3% of the states population. Of this population, 1,643,638 (13.1%) are non-Hispanic Black alone, while 28,321 identify as [Afro-Latino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afro-Latino), and 250,300 identify as Black in combination with any other race.[^cite_note-auto7-128][^cite_note-auto8-129][^cite_note-auto9-130] While most of the state's Black population identifies as African-American,[^cite_note-auto10-131] there are also 45,393 people of West Indian descent and 43,911 of Nigerian descent in the state.[^cite_note-auto1-132] A majority of the state's Black population resides in the Chicago metropolitan area, with 42% living in Chicago itself.[^cite_note-133]&#x20;

According to a study from the Brookings Institution, the Chicago area has the third highest level of black-white residential segregation in the United States.[^cite_note-\:4-134] While some areas, including many neighborhoods on the city's west side and south side, as well as many suburbs in the [Chicago Southland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Southland), are majority Black, most neighborhoods on the north and northwest sides of Chicago, as well as most of the northern and southwestern suburbs of the city, have very small Black populations.[^cite_note-135]&#x20;

Outside of Chicagoland, urban areas in Southern and Central Illinois, including the [Metro East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metro_East), [Peoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peoria,_Illinois), [Springfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Springfield,_Illinois), and [Decatur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decatur,_Illinois) also have significant Black populations. The counties with the highest share of Black residents in Illinois are [Pulaski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulaski_County,_Illinois) and [Alexander County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_County,_Illinois), located at the southern tip of the state along the banks of the Ohio and Mississippi rivers in the region known as "[Little Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Illinois#Origin_of_%22Little_Egypt%22_name)", with Black residents making up 32% of the population in both counties.[^cite_note-136]&#x20;

The per-capita income of Black residents, at \$30,295, is the second lowest of all major ethnic or racial groups in the state, after Hispanics of any race. However, Black Illinoisans have the highest overall poverty rate of all groups in the state, at 23%.[^cite_note-\:5-137] The median age of Black residents in Illinois is 36.8 years.[^cite_note-138]&#x20;

Illinois has a long history of milestones regarding [African American](2154.md) involvement in politics. It was the first state to elect a Black person to the U.S. [House of Representatives](19468510.md) in the post-reconstruction era, with the election of [Oscar De Priest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscar_Stanton_De_Priest) in 1928.[^cite_note-139] It was also the first state to elect a Black woman to the [US Senate](24909346.md), with the election of [Carol Moseley Braun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carol_Moseley_Braun) in 1992.[^cite_note-auto11-140] Illinois senator [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) would become the first Black president of the United States following his victory in the [2008 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_United_States_presidential_election).&#x20;

#### Asian-Americans

The third largest minority group in Illinois are Asian-Americans. In 2023, 894,048 Illinoisans identified as being Asian alone or in combination, making up 7.1% of the state's population.[^cite_note-141] 756,661 identified as Asian alone, making up 6.0% of the population.[^cite_note-auto8-129] The state's Asian-American population has grown rapidly in recent decades, from only 2.5% of the total population in 1990, to over 7% in 2023.&#x20;

About 37% of the state's Asian population is of [South Asian](21566765.md) origin, 31% are [East Asian](19605700.md), 29% are [Southeast Asian](28741.md) (Mostly Filipino), 1% are [Central Asian](6742.md), and 2% are of 'unspecified' Asian origins.[^cite_note-auto12-142] The largest specific Asian groups in the state are Indian (277,961), Filipino (184,508), Chinese (160,880), Korean (67,452), and Pakistani (50,508).[^cite_note-auto13-143] About 85% of the state's Asian-American population identifies as Asian alone, while 15% identify as multiracial. Illinoisans of South Asian ancestry are significantly less likely than other Asian-Americans in the state to report multiracial ancestry, with 94% of Indian-Americans and 90% of Pakistani-Americans in the state identifying as Asian alone, compared to 82% of Chinese-American residents, 73% of Filipino-American residents, and only 44% of the state's 33,000 Japanese-American residents.[^cite_note-auto14-144][^cite_note-auto13-143]&#x20;

The overwhelming majority of the state's Asian population resides in the [Chicago metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_metropolitan_area), mostly in suburban areas.[^cite_note-145] Suburban [DuPage County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DuPage_County,_Illinois) has the highest share of Asian residents in the state, at 14.7%.[^cite_note-146] While Asians aren't the majority in any municipality within Illinois, they make up a significant share of the population (between 20 and 40%) in multiple western and northern suburbs of Chicago, including [Naperville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naperville,_Illinois), [Schaumburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schaumburg,_Illinois), [Hoffman Estates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoffman_Estates,_Illinois), [Skokie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skokie,_Illinois), [Niles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niles,_Illinois), and [Morton Grove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morton_Grove,_Illinois). Additionally, the Chicago [community area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Community_areas_in_Chicago) of Armour Square, which includes the city's [Chinatown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinatown,_Chicago) neighborhood, is majority Asian at 63.7% of the population.[^cite_note-147] Asian residents have the highest per-capita income of any major ethnic or racial group in the state, at \$54,122, and the second lowest poverty rate, after non-Hispanic whites, at 10.4%.[^cite_note-\:6-148][^cite_note-\:7-149] The median age of single-race Asian residents in Illinois is 38.9 years.[^cite_note-150] Illinois senator [Tammy Duckworth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tammy_Duckworth), of [Thai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thai_Americans) descent, is one of only three Asian Americans currently serving in the [US Senate](24909346.md).[^cite_note-151]&#x20;

#### Native Americans

In 2023, 271,494 Illinoisans identified as Native American alone or in combination, making up 2.2% of the state's population. 184,155 Illinoisans identified as Native American in combination with any other race, while 87,339 identified as being Native American alone. Over 80% of the state's Native American population also identified as being Hispanic or Latino, with only 12,385, or 0.1% of the population, identifying as non-Hispanic Native American alone.[^cite_note-auto2-152][^cite_note-auto7-128] According to the 2023 American Community Survey, the largest self-identified Indigenous groups in the state were [Aztec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aztecs) (53,815) and [Maya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maya_peoples) (17,866).[^cite_note-153] The largest non-Latin American Indigenous groups in the state were [Blackfeet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blackfeet_Nation) (8,674) and [Navajo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navajo) (3,950).&#x20;

#### Pacific Islanders

The state has a very small number of [Pacific Islanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Islander_Americans), numbering 17,982 and making only 0.1% of the population in 2023.[^cite_note-154] The majority of Pacific Islanders in the state identify as multiracial, with only 5,852 identifying as Pacific Islander alone.[^cite_note-auto2-152] The largest Pacific Islander groups in the state are [Native Hawaiians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_Hawaiians) (6,362), [Samoans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samoans) (2,211), and [Chamorros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chamorro_people) (1,519).[^cite_note-155]&#x20;

#### European-Americans

In 2023, 7,260,529 Illinoisans identified as non-Hispanic white alone, making up 57.9% of the population.[^cite_note-auto5-113] Although a majority of white residents in the state live in the Chicago area, the counties with the highest percent share of white population are all located in rural areas with smaller populations, with non-Hispanic white people making up over 80 or 90% of the population in most non-urban counties in the state.[^cite_note-156]&#x20;

In the Chicago metro area, the white population is located mostly in suburban areas, with non-Hispanic white residents making up a majority of the population (between 53%[^cite_note-157] and 75%[^cite_note-158]) in every metropolitan county other than [Cook County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cook_County,_Illinois), while making up only 31.7% of the population in the city of Chicago itself.[^cite_note-159] Within Chicago, there is significant [racial segregation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racial_segregation), with the white population concentrated primarily on the [North Side](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Side,_Chicago) of the city, while many predominantly African-American or Hispanic neighborhoods on the [West Side](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Side,_Chicago) and [South Side](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Side,_Chicago) of the city have almost no non-Hispanic white residents.[^cite_note-160]&#x20;

The largest European ancestry reported in Illinois is [German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Americans). 1,983,050 Illinoisans, or 15.8% of the total population, identify with German ancestry, making it the single largest ancestry group in the state.[^cite_note-auto15-161] German ancestry predominates among the white population in every county in [northern Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Illinois), as well as most counties in the southwestern part of the state, while [English-Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_Americans), making up 6.8% of Illinois' population, predominate in most of the southeastern counties.[^cite_note-auto10-131] The state's white population also includes a large number of [Irish](775859.md) (10.5%), [Polish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Americans) (6.1%), and [Italian-Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Americans) (5.5%), with these groups concentrated mainly in Chicago and the surrounding suburbs.[^cite_note-162] Illinois' Polish-American population of 761,948 is the highest out of any state. The state is also home to a significant population of other [Eastern European](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Europe) ethnicities, also largely concentrated around the Chicago area, including 86,814 [Russians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Americans), 83,679 [Czechs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czech_Americans), 71,279 [Ukrainians](5938693.md), and 35,407 [Croatians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_Americans).[^cite_note-163][^cite_note-164]&#x20;

Most Illinoisans who report any European ancestry identify with multiple ancestries. Those of partial descent make up the majority of most European ancestry groups in Illinois, including 58% of Polish-Americans, 68% of English-Americans, 69% of German-Americans, and 75% of Irish-Americans in the state. Of all European ancestries numbering over 50,000 in Illinois, those identifying solely with that ancestry are only a majority among the state's 71,279 [Ukrainian-Americans](5938693.md), with two thirds of this group identifying as Ukrainian alone.[^cite_note-auto16-165][^cite_note-auto1-132]&#x20;

Along with white residents reporting multiple European ancestries, about 3% of the state's population identifies as non-Hispanic white in combination with another racial group.[^cite_note-\:9-102] Additionally, while those of Hispanic ethnicity are not distinguished between total and partial Hispanic origin, an estimated 1.7% of Illinois' population are Hispanics who report a non-Spanish European ancestry.[^cite_note-auto3-127]&#x20;

Non-Hispanic whites have the second highest per-capita income of any racial group in the state, at \$53,591, just slightly below that of Asians.[^cite_note-\:8-124] The white poverty rate, at only 8.3%, is the lowest of all major racial or ethnic groups in the state, including Asians.[^cite_note-\:7-149] The median age of non-Hispanic white residents is the oldest of all racial or ethnic groups in the state, at 44.2 years.[^cite_note-166]&#x20;

#### Arab Americans

The state is home to a significant Middle Eastern population, with 101,464 people (0.8% of the population) identifying as [Arab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Americans).***[^cite_note-167]***[^cite_note-auto1-132] 71,422, (0.6% of the population) identify as Arab alone.[^cite_note-auto16-165] The highest concentration of Arab-Americans in the state are found in the southern suburbs of Chicago, in the towns of [Brigeview](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bridgeview,_Illinois),[^cite_note-168] [Oak Lawn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oak_Lawn,_Illinois),[^cite_note-169] [Palos Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palos_Hills,_Illinois),[^cite_note-170] and [Hickory Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hickory_Hills,_Illinois), where they make up between 5–12% of the population.[^cite_note-171]&#x20;

Illinois has the largest [Palestinian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Americans) population in the United States. According to census estimates, 19,255 Illinoisans have Palestinian ancestry, while other estimates place the population of Palestinian descent in the Chicago area as high as 85,000.[^cite_note-172]&#x20;

#### Demographic Trends



Illinois [population pyramid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_pyramid)

As of 2023, 50% of Illinois's population younger than age 18 were minorities. This marks an increase from 2010, when 47% of children in the state were minorities, and 2000, when 41% were minorities.[^cite_note-173] (Note: Children born to white Hispanics or to a sole full or partial minority parent are counted as minorities. Arabs are classified as white in census data.).[^cite_note-174]&#x20;

The state's most populous ethnic group, non-Hispanic white, has declined from 83.5% in 1970[^cite_note-175] to 58.5% in 2022.[^cite_note-CensusACS2023-115] Almost 60% of Illinois' minority population, including over 67% of the black population, lives in [Cook County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cook_County,_Illinois), while the county includes about 40% of the state's total population.[^cite_note-176] Cook County, which is home to [Chicago](6886.md), is the only [majority-minority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majority_minority_in_the_United_States) county within Illinois, with non-Hispanic whites making up a plurality of 40.4% of the population.[^cite_note-177] Despite being the most ethnically diverse state in the Midwest, urban areas in Illinois have had a persistently high level of [racial segregation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racial_segregation), with a study from the [Brookings Institution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brookings_Institution) finding that the Chicago area has the third highest level of black-white [residential segregation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Residential_segregation) out of all major metropolitan areas in the United States.[^cite_note-\:4-134]&#x20;

### Ancestry

According to 2022 estimates from the American Community Survey, 16% of the population had [German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Americans) ancestry, 14% had [Mexican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_Americans) ancestry, 10.4% had [Irish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irish_Americans) ancestry, 7.1% had [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_Americans) ancestry, 6.2% had [Polish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Americans) ancestry, 5.2% had [Italian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Americans) ancestry, 3.4% listed themselves as [American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_ancestry), 2.3% had [Indian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Americans) ancestry, 1.7% had [Puerto Rican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puerto_Ricans) ancestry, 1.7% had [Swedish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swedish_Americans) ancestry, 1.4% had [Filipino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filipino_Americans) ancestry, 1.4% had [French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Americans) ancestry, and 1.2% had [Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Americans) ancestry. The state also has a large population of [African-Americans](2154.md), making up 15.3% of the population alone or in combination.[^cite_note-auto15-161][^cite_note-auto13-143][^cite_note-auto6-116][^cite_note-auto9-130] This table displays all self-reported [ancestries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancestries_of_Americans) with over 50,000 members in Illinois, alone or in combination, according to estimates from the 2022 American Community Survey. ### Immigration

At the 2022 estimates from the [U.S. Census Bureau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Census_Bureau), there were 1,810,100 foreign-born inhabitants of the state or 14.4% of the population, with 37.8% from Mexico or Central America, 31% from Asia, 20.2% from Europe, 4.3% from South America, 4.2% from Africa, 1% from Canada, and 0.2% from Oceania.[^cite_note-179][^cite_note-180] Of the foreign-born population, 53.5% were [naturalized U.S. citizens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_nationality_law), and 46.5% were not U.S. citizens.[^cite_note-181] The top countries of origin for immigrants in Illinois were [Mexico](3966054.md), [India](14533.md), [Poland](22936.md), the [Philippines](23440.md) and [China](5405.md) in 2018 and 2023.[^cite_note-182]&#x20;

### Age and sex

In 2022, 11.2% of Illinois's population was reported as being under the age of 9, 12.9% were between 10 and 19 years old, 13.4% were 20–29 years old, 13.6% were 30–39 years old, 12.6% were 40–49 years old, 12.7% were 50–59 years old, 11.9% were 60–69 years old, 7.7% were 70–79 years old, and 4% were over the age of 80.[^cite_note-Census_profile:_Illinois-185] As of 2023, 21.5% of the population is under the age of 18.[^cite_note-auto17-186] The median age in Illinois is 39.1 years.&#x20;

Females make up approximately 50.5% of the population, while males make up 49.5%.[^cite_note-auto17-186] According to a 2022 study from the [Williams Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Williams_Institute), an estimated 0.44% of adults in Illinois identify as [transgender](19904525.md), a rate slightly lower than the national estimate of 0.52%.[^cite_note-187] According to a Gallup survey from 2019, 4.3% of adults in Illinois identify as [LGBTQ](78174325.md).[^cite_note-188]&#x20;

### Socioeconomics

As of 2023, the [per-capita income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Per_capita_income) in Illinois is \$45,043,[^cite_note-189] and the [median income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Median_income) for a household in the state is \$80,306, slightly higher than the national average. 11.6% of the population lives below the [poverty line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty_in_the_United_States), including 15% of children under 18 and 11% of those over the age of 65. There is significant racial income inequality in the state, with Asians and Non-Hispanic Whites having a per-capita income almost double that of Black and Hispanic residents.&#x20;

| Race/Ethnicity | Per capita income (2023) | Poverty Rate (2023) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Asian[^cite_note-\:6-148] | \$54,122 | 10.4% |
| White (Non-Hispanic)[^cite_note-\:8-124][^cite_note-\:7-149] | \$53,591 | 8.3% |
| **All residents** | **\$45,043** | **11.6%** |
| Black[^cite_note-190][^cite_note-\:5-137] | \$30,295 | 23.0% |
| Hispanic (Any Race)[^cite_note-\:8-124][^cite_note-\:3-125] | \$28,541 | 14.1% |

There are 5,071,288 households in Illinois, with an average size of 2.4 people per household. 48% of the population over the age of 15 is married. As of 2023, Illinois' [total fertility rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Total_fertility_rate) is the 11th lowest of all US states, with a lifetime average of 1.50 births per woman, in comparison to an average of 1.64 on the national level.[^cite_note-2023cdc-191]&#x20;

90.6% of the adult population has a high school diploma, and 38.3% of the population over 25 has a bachelor's degree or higher, compared to a national average of 36.2%.[^cite_note-Census_profile:_Illinois-185]&#x20;

In 2022, Illinois scored 0.932 on the UN's [Human Development Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Development_Index), placing it in the category of "very high" Human Development and slightly higher than the US average of 0.927.[^cite_note-192]&#x20;

According to [HUD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Housing_and_Urban_Development)'s 2022 [Annual Homeless Assessment Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annual_Homeless_Assessment_Report_to_Congress), there were an estimated 9,212 [homeless people in Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homelessness_in_Illinois).[^cite_note-193][^cite_note-194]&#x20;

According to 2022 data from the [Prison Policy Initiative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prison_Policy_Initiative), an estimated 53,000 people were imprisoned in local jails, state prisons, federal prisons, or detention centers in the state, meaning that about 0.43% of the state's total population was incarcerated.[^cite_note-195] Census data from 2023 reports an estimated 59,254 people (0.47%) imprisoned in adult correctional facilities in the state.[^cite_note-196] However, Illinois' prison incarceration rate has declined by almost 50% since 2014, and the state has the 13th lowest total incarceration rate out of all 50 states.[^cite_note-197]&#x20;

### Languages

Main article: [Languages of Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_Illinois)

The [official language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_language) of Illinois is [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language),[^cite_note-208] although between 1923 and 1969, state law gave official status to "the American language". Nearly 80% of people in Illinois speak English natively, and most of the rest speak it fluently as a second language.[^cite_note-209] A number of dialects of [American English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_English) are spoken, ranging from [Inland Northern American English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inland_Northern_American_English) and [African-American English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African-American_English) around Chicago, to [Midland American English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midland_American_English) in Central Illinois, to [Southern American English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_American_English) in the far south.&#x20;

Over 24% of Illinoians speak a language other than English at home, of which [Spanish](26825.md) is by far the most widespread, at more than 14% of the total population.[^cite_note-210] A sizeable number of [Polish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_language) speakers is present in the [Chicago Metropolitan Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Metropolitan_Area). [Illinois Country French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Country_French) has mostly gone extinct in Illinois, although it is still celebrated in the [French Colonial Historic District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Colonial_Historic_District).&#x20;

#### Christianity

The religious demographics of Illinois closely mirror the religious demography of the nation as a whole. [Christians](18337522.md) of any denomination make up 62% of the population of Illinois, a share identical to the estimated national percentage of 62%, according to the Pew Research Center.[^cite_note-213]&#x20;

However, looking at specific denominations, Illinois has a significantly larger Catholic population than most states. Roman Catholics constitute the single largest religious denomination in Illinois; they are heavily concentrated in and around Chicago, reflecting the prominent Hispanic, Polish, Irish, and Italian diasporas in the area.[^cite_note-auto-214] As of 2023, Catholics account for nearly 25% of the state's population. In 2010, Catholics in Illinois numbered 3,648,907, while by 2020, this number had declined to 3,099,544.[^cite_note-auto-214] The first and only American-born Catholic [pope](23056.md), [Pope Leo XIV](54520372.md), was born in Chicago and raised in the suburb of [Dolton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolton,_Illinois).&#x20;

When taken together as a group, the various Protestant denominations comprise a greater percentage of the state's population than do Catholics, making up 36% of the state's population. The largest Protestant denominations in 2020 were the [United Methodist Church](32304.md) with 235,045 members and the [Southern Baptist Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Baptist_Convention) with 222,589. Illinois has one of the largest concentrations of [Missouri Synod Lutherans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missouri_Synod_Lutherans) in the United States, at 182,097.&#x20;

Illinois played an important role in the early [Latter Day Saint movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latter_Day_Saint_movement), with [Nauvoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nauvoo,_Illinois) becoming a gathering place for Mormons in the early 1840s. Following the 1844 [killing of Mormon leader Joseph Smith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_of_Joseph_Smith) by a lynch mob in nearby Carthage, Nauvoo was the location of the [succession crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Succession_crisis_\(Latter_Day_Saints\)), which led to the separation of the Mormon movement into [several Latter Day Saint sects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_denominations_in_the_Latter_Day_Saint_movement). [The Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Church_of_Jesus_Christ_of_Latter-day_Saints), the largest of the sects to emerge from the Mormon schism, has more than 55,000 adherents in Illinois today.[^cite_note-215]&#x20;

#### Other Abrahamic religious communities



[The Baháʼí House of Worship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_House_of_Worship_\(Wilmette,_Illinois\)) in [Wilmette, Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilmette,_Illinois)

A significant number of adherents of other [Abrahamic faiths](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abrahamic_religions) can be found in Illinois. Largely concentrated in the [Chicago metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_metropolitan_area), followers of the [Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim), [Baháʼí](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith), and [Jewish](25955086.md) religions all call the state home.[^cite_note-thearda.com-216] Muslims constituted the largest non-Christian group, with 473,792 adherents. Illinois has the largest concentration of Muslims by state in the country, with 3.7% of the population being Muslim.[^cite_note-auto18-217] The highest concentration of Muslims in the state is in suburban [DuPage county](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DuPage_County,_Illinois), where they make up approximately 7.6% of the population.[^cite_note-218] In the Chicago area as a whole, about 4.7% of the population are Muslim, the highest rate of any major metropolitan area in the United States.[^cite_note-auto18-217]&#x20;

The largest and oldest surviving [Baháʼí House of Worship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_House_of_Worship_\(Wilmette,_Illinois\)) in the world is located on the shores of [Lake Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Michigan) in [Wilmette, Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilmette,_Illinois), one of eight continental [Baháʼí House of Worship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_House_of_Worship).[^cite_note-219] It serves as a space for people of all backgrounds and religions to gather, meditate, reflect, and pray, expressing the [Baháʼí](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith) principle of the [oneness of religions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith_and_the_unity_of_religion).[^cite_note-220]&#x20;

The Chicago area has a very large Jewish community, particularly in the northern suburbs of [Skokie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skokie,_Illinois), [Buffalo Grove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buffalo_Grove,_Illinois), [Highland Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Highland_Park,_Illinois), and surrounding areas. Former Chicago Mayor [Rahm Emanuel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rahm_Emanuel) was the Windy City's first Jewish mayor. The current governor of Illinois, [J.B. Pritzker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JB_Pritzker), is Jewish, being the third person of Jewish descent to hold the office after [Henry Horner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Horner) and [Samuel H. Shapiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samuel_Shapiro_\(Illinois_politician\)).[^cite_note-221] Almost 4% of the Chicago area's population is Jewish, numbering over 300,000 people.[^cite_note-222]&#x20;

#### Other religions

Chicago is also home to a significant population of [Hindus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism), [Sikhs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sikh), [Jains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jainism), and [Buddhists](3267529.md).[^cite_note-thearda.com-216]&#x20;

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Illinois)

See also: [List of Illinois locations by per capita income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Illinois_locations_by_per_capita_income)



Illinois counties by GDP (2021)

In 2025, the [gross state product](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_by_GDP_\(nominal\)) for Illinois was \$1.201 trillion and the state's per capita personal income was \$77,772.[^cite_note-223] Illinois has the 18th-largest economy in the world.[^cite_note-224] In 2025, 99.6% of businesses in the state were [small businesses](276495.md), and employed 43.7% of Illinois's workforce.[^cite_note-225] As of May 2025, the [unemployment rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unemployment_rate) in Illinois was 4.8%.[^cite_note-226]&#x20;

As of 2026, Illinois's [minimum wage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minimum_wage_in_the_United_States) is \$15.00 per hour for employees over the age of 18. Tipped workers are to be paid a minimum of 60% of minimum wage.[^cite_note-227]&#x20;

### Agriculture

Main article: [Agriculture in Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Illinois)



A [John Deere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Deere) [combine harvester](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combine_harvester) on an Illinois farm; the company is headquartered in [Moline, Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moline,_Illinois).

Illinois's major agricultural outputs are [corn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maize), [soybeans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soybean), [hogs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pig), [cattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cattle), [dairy products](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dairy_product), and wheat. In most years, Illinois is either the first or second state for the highest production of soybeans. For example, Illinois had a harvest of 648.9 million bushels (17.7 million [metric tons](31185.md)) in 2023, ahead of Iowa's production of 573 million bushels (15.6 million metric tons).[^cite_note-228] Illinois ranks second in U.S. corn production with more than 1.5 billion bushels produced annually.[^cite_note-ilcorn-229] With a production capacity of 1.5 billion gallons per year, Illinois is a top producer of ethanol, ranking third in the United States in 2011.[^cite_note-eia.gov-230] Illinois is a leader in food manufacturing and meat processing.[^cite_note-stateil-231] Although Chicago may no longer be "[Hog Butcher for the World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_\(poem\))", the Chicago area remains a global center for [food manufacture and meat processing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_manufacturers_of_Chicago),[^cite_note-stateil-231] with many plants, processing houses, and distribution facilities concentrated in the area of the former [Union Stock Yards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Stock_Yards).[^cite_note-232] Illinois also produces [wine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_wine), and the state is home to two [American viticultural areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_viticultural_area). In the area of The Meeting of the Great Rivers Scenic Byway, peaches and apples are grown. The German immigrants from agricultural backgrounds who settled in Illinois in the mid- to late 19th century are in part responsible for the profusion of fruit orchards in that area of Illinois.[^cite_note-233] Illinois's universities are actively researching alternative agricultural products as alternative crops.&#x20;

### Manufacturing



[Rivian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rivian) Automotive Plant in [Normal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Normal,_Illinois).

Illinois is one of the nation's manufacturing leaders, boasting annual value added productivity by manufacturing of over \$107 billion in 2006. As of 2011, Illinois is ranked as the 4th-most productive manufacturing state in the country, behind California, Texas, and Ohio.[^cite_note-234] About three-quarters of the state's manufacturers are located in the Northeastern Opportunity Return Region, with 38 percent of Illinois's approximately 18,900 manufacturing plants located in Cook County. As of 2006, the leading manufacturing industries in Illinois, based upon value-added, were chemical manufacturing (\$18.3 billion), machinery manufacturing (\$13.4 billion), food manufacturing (\$12.9 billion), fabricated metal products (\$11.5 billion), transportation equipment (\$7.4 billion), plastics and rubber products (\$7.0 billion), and computer and electronic products (\$6.1 billion).[^cite_note-235]&#x20;

Some of the largest manufacturing facilities in Illinois include [Rivian Automotive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rivian) in [Normal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Normal,_Illinois), [Ford Motor Company](30433662.md) in Chicago, [Stellantis North America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stellantis_North_America) in [Belvidere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belvidere_Assembly_Plant), and [Lion Electric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lion_Electric_Company) in Joliet.[^cite_note-236]&#x20;

### Services



The [Federal Reserve Bank of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Reserve_Bank_of_Chicago), one of twelve [Federal Reserve Banks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Reserve_Bank), at the heart of Chicago's financial center.



[Trading floor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trading_floor) in the [Chicago Board of Trade Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Board_of_Trade_Building)

By the early 2000s, Illinois's economy had moved toward a dependence on high-value-added services, such as financial trading, higher education, law, logistics, and medicine. In some cases, these services clustered around institutions that hearkened back to Illinois's earlier economies. For example, the [Chicago Mercantile Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Mercantile_Exchange), a trading exchange for global [derivatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Derivative_\(finance\)), had begun its life as an agricultural [futures market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Futures_exchange). The Chicago Mercantile Exchange merged with the [Chicago Board of Trade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Board_of_Trade) (CBOT) to form [CME Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CME_Group), one of the world's leading derivatives marketplaces and [futures exchanges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Futures_exchange). CME Group owns the [New York Mercantile Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_Mercantile_Exchange) and owns 27% of [S\&P Dow Jones Indices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S%26P_Dow_Jones_Indices). [Cboe Global Markets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cboe_Global_Markets) is the largest exchange for trading in [options](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Option_\(finance\)) in the United States and the third-largest exchange for trading in [equities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equities). Other important non-manufacturing industries include publishing, tourism, and energy production and distribution.[^cite_note-237]&#x20;

Illinois is home to the headquarters of many [Fortune 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_500) and Global 500 companies. They include [Abbott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abbott_Laboratories), [Abbvie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AbbVie), [Ace Hardware](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ace_Hardware), [Allstate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allstate), [Archer Daniels Midland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archer_Daniels_Midland), [Baxter International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baxter_International), [CDW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CDW), [Exelon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exelon), [GE Healthcare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GE_HealthCare), [Grainger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/W._W._Grainger), [John Deere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Deere), [Kraft Heinz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraft_Heinz), [McDonald's](2480627.md), [Motorola Solutions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motorola_Solutions), [Mondelez International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mondelez_International), [State Farm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Farm), [Ulta Beauty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ulta_Beauty), [United Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Airlines), and [Walgreens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walgreens). Other notable companies include [Aldi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aldi), [BMO Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BMO_Bank), [Conagra Brands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conagra_Brands), [Country Financial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country_Financial), [Dude Wipes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dude_Wipes), [Groupon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Groupon), [Growmark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Growmark), [Grubhub](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grubhub), [Hyatt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyatt), [Jimmy John's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmy_John%27s), [JLL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JLL_\(company\)), [Medline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medline_Inc.), [Morningstar, Inc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morningstar,_Inc.), [Sears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sears), and [US Foods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Foods). Until 2022, Illinois was also home to the headquarters of [Boeing](18933266.md), [Caterpillar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caterpillar_Inc.), [Citadel LLC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citadel_LLC), and [Tyson Foods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyson_Foods).[^cite_note-238]&#x20;

### Investments

Venture capitalists funded a total of approximately \$62 billion in the U.S. economy in 2016. Of this amount, Illinois-based companies received approximately \$1.1 billion. Similarly, in FY 2016, the federal government spent \$461 billion on contracts in the U.S. Of this amount, Illinois-based companies received approximately \$8.7 billion.&#x20;

### Energy

See also: [List of power stations in Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_power_stations_in_Illinois) and [Solar power in Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_power_in_Illinois)

Illinois is a net importer of fuels for energy, despite large coal resources and some minor oil production. Illinois exports electricity, ranking fifth among states in electricity production and seventh in electricity consumption.[^cite_note-finley-239]&#x20;

#### Coal



Location of the [Illinois Basin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Basin)

The coal industry of Illinois has its origins in the middle 19th century, when entrepreneurs such as [Jacob Loose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacob_Loose) discovered coal in locations such as [Sangamon County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sangamon_County). [Jacob Bunn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacob_Bunn) contributed to the development of the Illinois coal industry. Coal mining in central and southern Illinois was important in the late 19th and early 20th centuries. The [1909 Cherry Mine disaster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1909_Cherry_Mine_disaster) was an underground fire that killed 259 miners in the third worst disaster in the history of American coal mining. After 1940 there was a slow decline in coal mining.[^cite_note-240] About 68% of Illinois has coal-bearing strata of the [Pennsylvanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennsylvanian_\(geology\)) geologic period. According to the Illinois State Geological Survey, 211 billion tons of [bituminous coal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bituminous_coal) are estimated to lie under the surface, having a total heating value greater than the estimated oil deposits in the [Arabian Peninsula](47858.md).[^cite_note-241] However, this coal has a high [sulfur](27127.md) content, which causes [acid rain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acid_rain), unless special equipment is used to reduce [sulfur dioxide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sulfur_dioxide) [emissions](10934212.md).[^cite_note-Nelson-27][^cite_note-Biles-30][^cite_note-Horsley-69] Many Illinois [power plants](212141.md) are not equipped to burn high-sulfur coal. In 1999, Illinois produced 40.4 million tons of coal, but only 17 million tons (42%) of Illinois coal was consumed in Illinois. Most of the coal produced in Illinois is exported to other states and countries. In 2008, Illinois exported three million tons of coal and was projected to export nine million in 2011, as demand for energy grows in places such as China, India, and elsewhere in Asia and Europe.[^cite_note-242] As of 2010, Illinois was ranked third in recoverable coal reserves at producing mines in the nation.[^cite_note-eia.gov-230] Most of the coal produced in Illinois is exported to other states, while much of the coal burned for power in Illinois (21 million tons in 1998) is mined in the [Powder River Basin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Powder_River_Basin) of [Wyoming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wyoming).[^cite_note-finley-239]&#x20;

#### Petroleum

Illinois is a leading refiner of petroleum in the American [Midwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midwestern_United_States), with a combined crude oil distillation capacity of nearly 900,000 bbl/d (140,000 m<sup>3</sup>/d). However, Illinois has very limited crude oil proved reserves that account for less than 1% of the U.S. total reserves. Residential heating is 81% natural gas compared to less than 1% [heating oil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heating_oil). Illinois is ranked 14th in [oil production](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_oil-producing_states#North_America) among states, with a daily output of approximately 28,000 bbl (4,500 m<sup>3</sup>) in 2005.[^cite_note-EIA_petro-243][^cite_note-244]&#x20;

#### Nuclear power

Main article: [Nuclear power in the United States](4131402.md)



[Byron Nuclear Generating Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byron_Nuclear_Generating_Station) in [Ogle County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ogle_County,_Illinois)

Worldwide [nuclear power](22153.md) began in Illinois during 1942 with the [Chicago Pile-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Pile-1), the world's first artificial self-sustaining [nuclear chain reaction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_chain_reaction) in the world's first [nuclear reactor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_reactor_technology), built on the [University of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Chicago) campus. There are six operating [nuclear power plants](207347.md) in Illinois: [Braidwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Braidwood_Nuclear_Generating_Station), [Byron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byron_Nuclear_Generating_Station), [Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clinton_Nuclear_Generating_Station), [Dresden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dresden_Nuclear_Power_Plant), [LaSalle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LaSalle_County_Generating_Station), and [Quad Cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quad_Cities_Nuclear_Generating_Station).[^cite_note-245] With the exception of the single-unit Clinton plant, each of these facilities has two reactors. Three reactors have been permanently shut down and are in various stages of decommissioning: [Dresden-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dresden-1) and [Zion-1 and 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zion_Nuclear_Power_Station). Illinois ranked first in the nation in 2010 in both nuclear capacity and nuclear generation. Generation from its nuclear power plants accounted for 12 percent of the nation's total.[^cite_note-eia.gov-230] In 2007, 48% of Illinois's electricity was generated using nuclear power.[^cite_note-EIA_glance-246] The [Morris Operation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morris_Operation) is the only de facto high-level [radioactive waste](37257.md) storage site in the United States.&#x20;

#### Wind power

Main article: [Wind power in Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wind_power_in_Illinois)



Average annual wind power distribution for Illinois, 50 m (160 ft) height above ground (2009)

Illinois has seen growing interest in the use of [wind power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wind_power) for electrical generation.[^cite_note-247] Most of Illinois was rated in 2009 as "marginal or fair" for wind energy production by the [U.S. Department of Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Department_of_Energy), with some western sections rated "good" and parts of the south rated "poor".[^cite_note-248] These ratings are for wind turbines with 50 m (160 ft) hub heights; newer wind turbines are taller, enabling them to reach [stronger winds farther from the ground](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wind_profile_power_law). As a result, more areas of Illinois have become prospective wind farm sites. As of September 2009, Illinois had 1116.06 [MW](21347693.md) of installed wind power [nameplate capacity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nameplate_capacity) with another 741.9 MW under construction.[^cite_note-awea_projects_illinois-249] Illinois ranked ninth among U.S. states in installed wind power capacity and sixteenth by potential capacity.[^cite_note-awea_projects_illinois-249] Large [wind farms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wind_farm) in Illinois include [Twin Groves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twin_Groves_Wind_Farm), [Rail Splitter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_Splitter_Wind_Farm), [EcoGrove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acciona_Energy), and [Mendota Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mendota_Hills_Wind_Farm).[^cite_note-awea_projects_illinois-249]&#x20;

As of 2007, wind energy represented only 1.7% of Illinois's energy production, and it was estimated that wind power could provide 5–10% of the state's energy needs.[^cite_note-250][^cite_note-251] Also, the [Illinois General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_General_Assembly) mandated in 2007 that by 2025, 25% of all electricity generated in Illinois is to come from [renewable resources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renewable_resource).[^cite_note-252]&#x20;

#### Biofuels

Illinois is ranked second in [corn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maize) production among U.S. states, and Illinois corn is used to produce 40% of the [ethanol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethanol) consumed in the United States.[^cite_note-ilcorn-229] The [Archer Daniels Midland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archer_Daniels_Midland) corporation in [Decatur, Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decatur,_Illinois), is the world's leading producer of ethanol from corn.&#x20;

The National Corn-to-Ethanol Research Center (NCERC), the world's only facility dedicated to researching the ways and means of converting corn (maize) to ethanol is located on the campus of [Southern Illinois University Edwardsville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Illinois_University_Edwardsville).[^cite_note-253][^cite_note-254]&#x20;

[University of Illinois Urbana-Champaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Illinois_Urbana-Champaign) is one of the partners in the [Energy Biosciences Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_Biosciences_Institute) (EBI), a \$500 million biofuels research project funded by petroleum giant [BP](18998720.md).[^cite_note-255][^cite_note-256]&#x20;

### Taxes

Tax is collected by the [Illinois Department of Revenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Department_of_Revenue). State [income tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Income_tax) is calculated by multiplying [net income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Net_income) by a [flat rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flat_fee). In 1990, that rate was set at 3%, but in 2010, the General Assembly voted for a temporary increase in the rate to 5%; the new rate went into effect on January 1, 2011; the personal income rate partially sunset on January 1, 2015, to 3.75%, while the corporate income tax fell to 5.25%.[^cite_note-257][^cite_note-258] Illinois failed to pass a budget from 2015 to 2017, after the 736-day [budget impasse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Budget_Impasse), a budget was passed in Illinois after lawmakers overturned Governor Bruce Rauner's veto; this budget raised the personal income rate to 4.95% and the corporate rate to 7%.[^cite_note-259] There are two rates for state [sales tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sales_tax): 6.25% for general merchandise and 1% for qualifying food, drugs, and medical appliances.[^cite_note-260] The [property tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Property_tax) is a major source of tax revenue for local government taxing districts. The property tax is a local—not state—tax imposed by local government taxing districts, which include counties, [townships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_township), municipalities, [school districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/School_district), and special taxation districts. The property tax in Illinois is imposed only on [real property](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_property).[^cite_note-Nelson-27][^cite_note-Biles-30][^cite_note-Horsley-69]&#x20;

On May 1, 2019, the Illinois Senate voted to approve a [constitutional amendment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Fair_Tax) that would have stricken language from the [Illinois Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Illinois) requiring a flat state income tax, in a 73–44 vote. If approved, the amendment would have allowed the state legislature to impose a graduated income tax based on annual income. The governor, [J. B. Pritzker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._B._Pritzker), approved the bill on May 27, 2019. It was scheduled for a 2020 general election ballot vote[^cite_note-1\:a-261][^cite_note-1\:b-262] and required 60 percent voter approval to effectively amend the state constitution.[^cite_note-1\:d-263] The amendment was not approved by Illinoisans, with 55.1% of voters voting "No" on approval and 44.9% voting "Yes".[^cite_note-264]&#x20;

As of 2017 Chicago had the highest state and local sales tax rate for a U.S. city with a populations above 200,000, at 10.250%.[^cite_note-265] The state of Illinois has the second highest rate of real estate tax: 2.31%, which is second only to New Jersey at 2.44%.[^cite_note-266]&#x20;

[Toll roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toll_road) are a *de facto* user tax on the citizens and visitors to the state of Illinois. Illinois ranks seventh out of the 11 states with the most miles of toll roads, at 282.1 miles. Chicago ranks fourth in most expensive toll roads in America by the mile, with the [Chicago Skyway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Skyway) charging 51.2 cents per mile.[^cite_note-267] Illinois also has the 11th highest gasoline tax by state, at 37.5 cents per gallon.[^cite_note-268]&#x20;

## Culture

### Museums

For a more comprehensive list, see [List of museums in Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_museums_in_Illinois).

Illinois has numerous museums; the greatest concentration of these are in Chicago. Several museums in Chicago are ranked as some of the best in the world. These include the [John G. Shedd Aquarium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shedd_Aquarium), the [Field Museum of Natural History](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Field_Museum_of_Natural_History), the [Art Institute of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_Institute_of_Chicago), the [Adler Planetarium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adler_Planetarium), and the [Museum of Science and Industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_Science_and_Industry_\(Chicago\)).&#x20;

The modern [Abraham Lincoln Presidential Library and Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham_Lincoln_Presidential_Library_and_Museum) in Springfield is the largest and most attended [presidential library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_library_system) in the country. The [Barack Obama Presidential Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama_Presidential_Center) opened in June 2026, located in Jackson Park in Chicago. The Illinois State Museum boasts a collection of 13.5 million objects that tell the story of Illinois life, land, people, and art. The ISM is among only 5% of the nation's museums that are accredited by the American Alliance of Museums. Other historical museums in the state include the [Polish Museum of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Museum_of_America) in [Chicago](6886.md); [Magnolia Manor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magnolia_Manor_\(Cairo,_Illinois\)) in [Cairo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cairo,_Illinois); [Easley Pioneer Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Easley_Pioneer_Museum) in [Ipava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ipava,_Illinois); the [Elihu Benjamin Washburne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elihu_Benjamin_Washburne_House); [Ulysses S. Grant Homes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ulysses_S._Grant_Home), both in [Galena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galena,_Illinois); and the Chanute Air Museum, located on the former Chanute Air Force Base in Rantoul.&#x20;

The Chicago metropolitan area also hosts two zoos: The [Brookfield Zoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brookfield_Zoo), located about ten miles west of the city center in suburban [Brookfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brookfield,_Illinois), contains more than 2,300 animals and covers 216 acres (87 ha). [Lincoln Park Zoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_Park_Zoo) is located in [Lincoln Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_Park) on Chicago's North Side, approximately 3 miles (4.8 km) north of the [Loop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Chicago). The zoo, including [Nature Boardwalk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nature_Boardwalk), occupies 49 acres (20 ha) of the park.&#x20;
