### Music

Main article: [Music of Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Illinois)

Illinois is a leader in music education, having hosted the [Midwest Clinic International Band and Orchestra Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midwest_Clinic) since 1946, as well being home to the Illinois Music Educators Association (ILMEA, formerly IMEA), one of the largest professional music educator's organizations in the country. Each summer since 2004, [Southern Illinois University Carbondale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Illinois_University_Carbondale) has played host to the Southern Illinois Music Festival, which presents dozens of performances throughout the region. Past featured artists include the [Eroica Trio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eroica_Trio) and violinist [David Kim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Kim_\(violinist\)).&#x20;

[Chicago](6886.md) is a [major center for music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Chicago)[^cite_note-269] in the [midwestern United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midwestern_United_States) where distinctive forms of blues (greatly responsible for the future creation of [rock and roll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_and_roll)), and [house music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_music), a genre of electronic dance music, were developed.&#x20;

The [Great Migration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Migration_\(African_American\)) of poor black workers from the South into the industrial cities brought traditional [jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazz) and [blues music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blues_music) to the city, resulting in [Chicago blues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_blues) and "Chicago-style" [Dixieland jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dixieland_jazz). Notable blues artists included [Muddy Waters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muddy_Waters), [Junior Wells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_Wells), [Howlin' Wolf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Howlin%27_Wolf) and both [Sonny Boy Williamsons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonny_Boy_Williamson_II); jazz greats included [Nat King Cole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nat_King_Cole), [Gene Ammons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gene_Ammons), [Benny Goodman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benny_Goodman), and [Bud Freeman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bud_Freeman). Chicago is also well known for its [soul music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soul_music).&#x20;

In the early 1930s, [Gospel music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gospel_music) began to gain popularity in Chicago due to [Thomas A. Dorsey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_A._Dorsey)'s contributions at [Pilgrim Baptist Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pilgrim_Baptist_Church).&#x20;

In the 1980s and 1990s, [heavy rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hard_rock), [punk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punk_rock), and [hip-hop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hip-hop) also became popular in Chicago. [Orchestras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orchestra) in Chicago include the [Chicago Symphony Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Symphony_Orchestra), the [Lyric Opera of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyric_Opera_of_Chicago), and the [Chicago Sinfonietta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Sinfonietta).[^cite_note-270]&#x20;

### Movies

[John Hughes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Hughes_\(filmmaker\)), who moved from [Grosse Pointe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grosse_Pointe,_Michigan) to [Northbrook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northbrook,_Illinois), based many films of his in [Chicago](6886.md) and its suburbs. *[Ferris Bueller's Day Off](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferris_Bueller%27s_Day_Off)*, *[Home Alone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Home_Alone)*, *[The Breakfast Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Breakfast_Club)*, and all his films take place in the fictional Shermer, Illinois. (The original name of Northbrook was Shermerville, and Hughes's High School, [Glenbrook North High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glenbrook_North_High_School), is on Shermer Road.) Most locations in his films include Glenbrook North, the former [Maine North High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maine_North_High_School), the [Ben Rose House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Rose_House) in Highland Park, and the famous Home Alone house in [Winnetka, Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winnetka,_Illinois).&#x20;

### Recreation

For a more comprehensive list, see [List of protected areas of Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_protected_areas_of_Illinois).



Sunset on the [Garden of the Gods Wilderness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garden_of_the_Gods_Wilderness), part of the [Shawnee National Forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shawnee_National_Forest) in southern Illinois.

The [Illinois state parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_state_parks) system began in 1908 with what is now [Fort Massac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Massac) State Park, becoming the first park in a system encompassing more than 60 parks and about the same number of recreational and wildlife areas.&#x20;

Areas under the protection of the [National Park Service](161535.md) include: the [Illinois and Michigan Canal National Heritage Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_and_Michigan_Canal) near [Lockport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockport,_Illinois),[^cite_note-271] the [Lewis and Clark National Historic Trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lewis_and_Clark_National_Historic_Trail), the [Lincoln Home National Historic Site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_Home_National_Historic_Site) in Springfield, the [Mormon Pioneer National Historic Trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mormon_Trail), the [Trail of Tears National Historic Trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trail_of_Tears), the [American Discovery Trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Discovery_Trail),[^cite_note-272] the [Pullman National Monument](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pullman_National_Monument), and [New Philadelphia Town Site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Philadelphia_Town_Site). The federal government also manages the [Shawnee National Forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shawnee_National_Forest) and the [Midewin National Tallgrass Prairie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midewin_National_Tallgrass_Prairie).&#x20;

### Sports

For a more comprehensive list, see [List of professional sports teams in Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_professional_sports_teams_in_Illinois) and [Sports in Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_in_Chicago).



[Soldier Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soldier_Field) is home to the [National Football League](21211.md)'s [Chicago Bears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Bears)



[Wrigley Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wrigley_Field) is home to [Major League Baseball](38776.md)'s [Chicago Cubs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Cubs)

As one of the United States' major metropolises, all [major sports leagues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_professional_sports_leagues_in_the_United_States_and_Canada) have teams headquartered in Chicago.&#x20;

Two [Major League Baseball](38776.md) teams are located in the state. The [Chicago Cubs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Cubs) of the [National League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_League_\(baseball\)) play in the second-oldest major league stadium, [Wrigley Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wrigley_Field), and went the longest length of time without a championship in all of major American sport, from 1908 to 2016, when they won the [World Series](23489161.md).[^cite_note-273][^cite_note-274] The [Chicago White Sox](5945.md) of the [American League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_League) won the World Series in 2005, their first since 1917. They play on the city's south side at [Rate Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rate_Field). The [Chicago Bears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Bears) football team has won nine total [NFL Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_NFL_champions), the last occurring in [Super Bowl XX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_XX) on January 26, 1986. The [Chicago Bulls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Bulls) of the [NBA](22093.md) is one of the most recognized [basketball teams](3921.md) in the world, largely as a result of the efforts of [Michael Jordan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Jordan), who led the team to six NBA championships in eight seasons in the 1990s. The [Chicago Blackhawks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Blackhawks) of the [NHL](21809.md) began playing in [1926](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1926%E2%80%9327_NHL_season) and became a member of the [Original Six](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Original_Six) once the NHL dropped to that number of teams during World War II. The Blackhawks have won six [Stanley Cups](66968.md), most recently in [2015](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014%E2%80%9315_NHL_season). [Chicago Fire FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Fire_FC) is a member of [MLS](71802.md) and has been one of the league's most successful and best-supported clubs since its founding in 1997, winning one league and four [Lamar Hunt U.S. Open Cups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lamar_Hunt_U.S._Open_Cup) in that timespan. The team played in [Bridgeview](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bridgeview,_Illinois), adjacent to Chicago from 2006 to 2019. The team now plays at Soldier Field in Chicago.&#x20;

[Chicago Stars FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Stars_FC), known in the past as the Chicago Red Stars, has played at the top level of U.S. women's soccer since its formation in 2009, except in the 2011 season. The team currently plays in the [National Women's Soccer League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Women%27s_Soccer_League) at [Martin Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Stadium_\(Northwestern_University\)) on the campus of [Northwestern University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwestern_University). The [Chicago Sky](2717051.md) have played in the [Women's National Basketball Association](57721.md) (WNBA) since 2006. The Sky won their first WNBA Championship in 2021. They play at [Wintrust Arena](40651728.md) in Chicago. The [Chicago Bandits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Bandits) of the [NPF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Pro_Fastpitch), a women's softball league, have won four league titles, most recently in 2016. They play at [Parkway Bank Sports Complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parkway_Bank_Sports_Complex) in [Rosemont, Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosemont,_Illinois), in the Chicago area.&#x20;

Many [minor league](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minor_league) teams also call Illinois their home. They include the [Bloomington Edge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bloomington_Edge) of the [Indoor Football League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indoor_Football_League), [Bloomington Flex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bloomington_Flex) of the [Midwest Professional Basketball Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midwest_Professional_Basketball_Association), [Chicago Dogs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Dogs) of the [American Association of Professional Baseball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Association_of_Professional_Baseball), [Chicago Fire FC II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Fire_FC_II) of [MLS Next Pro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MLS_Next_Pro), [Chicago Wolves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Wolves) of the [American Hockey League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Hockey_League), [Gateway Grizzlies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gateway_Grizzlies) of the [Frontier League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frontier_League), [Kane County Cougars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kane_County_Cougars) of the American Association, [Joliet Slammers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joliet_Slammers) of the Frontier League, [Peoria Chiefs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peoria_Chiefs) of the [Midwest League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midwest_League), [Peoria Rivermen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peoria_Rivermen_\(SPHL\)) of the [SPHL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Professional_Hockey_League), [Rockford Aviators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rockford_Aviators) of the Frontier League, [Rockford IceHogs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rockford_IceHogs) of the AHL, [Schaumburg Boomers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schaumburg_Boomers) of the Frontier League, [Southern Illinois Miners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Illinois_Miners) in the Frontier League, [Windy City Bulls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Windy_City_Bulls) of the [NBA G League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBA_G_League), and [Windy City ThunderBolts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Windy_City_ThunderBolts) of the Frontier League.&#x20;



[Gies Memorial Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gies_Memorial_Stadium) and [State Farm Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Farm_Center) at the [University of Illinois Urbana-Champaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Illinois_Urbana-Champaign)

The state features 13 athletic programs that compete in [NCAA Division I](669931.md), the highest level of U.S. college sports. The two most prominent are the [Illinois Fighting Illini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Fighting_Illini) and [Northwestern Wildcats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwestern_Wildcats), both members of the [Big Ten Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Ten_Conference) and the only ones competing in the "[Power Four conferences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Power_conferences)". The Fighting Illini football team has won five national championships and three [Rose Bowl Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rose_Bowl_Game), whereas the men's basketball team has won 17 conference seasons and played five Final Fours. Meanwhile, the Wildcats have won eight football conference championships and one Rose Bowl Game. The [Northern Illinois Huskies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Illinois_Huskies) returned to the [Horizon League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horizon_League) in 2026 after an absence of nearly 30 years; with that conference not sponsoring football, the Huskies moved that sport to the [Mountain West Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain_West_Conference). During their most recent tenure in the [Mid-American Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mid-American_Conference) (1997–2026), the Huskies won four conference championships and earning a bid in the [Orange Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orange_Bowl). Five schools have football programs that compete in the second level of Division I football, the [Football Championship Subdivision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_Championship_Subdivision). The [Illinois State Redbirds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_State_Redbirds) and [Southern Illinois Salukis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Illinois_Salukis) are members of the [Missouri Valley Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missouri_Valley_Conference) for non-football sports and the [Missouri Valley Football Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missouri_Valley_Football_Conference). The [Eastern Illinois Panthers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Illinois_Panthers) and [Western Illinois Leathernecks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Illinois_Leathernecks) are members of the [Ohio Valley Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio_Valley_Conference). The [Chicago State Cougars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_State_Cougars) are playing their first season of varsity football in 2026 as an [FCS independent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NCAA_Division_I_FCS_independent_schools); after that season, the football team will join the rest of the Cougars' sports in the NEC (historically known as the [Northeast Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeast_Conference)). The city of Chicago is home to three Division I programs that do not sponsor football: the [DePaul Blue Demons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DePaul_Blue_Demons) of the [Big East Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_East_Conference), [Loyola Ramblers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loyola_Ramblers) of the [Atlantic 10 Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_10_Conference), and [UIC Flames](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UIC_Flames) of the Missouri Valley Conference. Finally, two non-football Division I programs are located downstate. The [Bradley Braves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bradley_Braves) are Missouri Valley Conference members, and the [SIU Edwardsville Cougars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SIU_Edwardsville_Cougars) compete in the Ohio Valley Conference.&#x20;



[Chicagoland Speedway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicagoland_Speedway)

[Motor racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motor_racing) oval tracks at the [Chicagoland Speedway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicagoland_Speedway) in [Joliet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joliet,_Illinois), the [Chicago Motor Speedway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Motor_Speedway) in [Cicero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cicero,_Illinois) and the [Gateway Motorsports Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gateway_Motorsports_Park) in [Madison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madison,_Illinois), near St. Louis, have hosted [NASCAR](21114.md), [CART](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Championship_Auto_Racing_Teams), and [IRL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indy_Racing_League) races, whereas the [Sports Car Club of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_Car_Club_of_America), among other national and regional [road racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Road_racing) clubs, have visited the [Autobahn Country Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autobahn_Country_Club) in Joliet, the [Blackhawk Farms Raceway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blackhawk_Farms_Raceway) in [South Beloit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Beloit,_Illinois) and the former [Meadowdale International Raceway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meadowdale_International_Raceway) in [Carpentersville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carpentersville,_Illinois). Illinois also has several [short tracks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Short_track_motor_racing) and [dragstrips](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dragstrip). The dragstrip at Gateway International Raceway and the [Route 66 Raceway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Route_66_Raceway), which sits on the same property as the Chicagoland Speedway, both host [NHRA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NHRA) drag races.&#x20;

Illinois features several golf courses, such as [Olympia Fields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympia_Fields_Country_Club), [Medinah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medinah_Country_Club), [Midlothian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midlothian_Country_Club), [Cog Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cog_Hill_Golf_%26_Country_Club), and [Conway Farms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conway_Farms_Golf_Club), which have often hosted the [BMW Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BMW_Championship_\(PGA_Tour\)), [Western Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Open), and [Women's Western Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women%27s_Western_Open). Also, the state has hosted 13 editions of the [U.S. Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Open_\(golf\)) (latest at Olympia Fields in 2003), six editions of the [PGA Championship](224616.md) (latest at Medinah in 2006), three editions of the [U.S. Women's Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Women%27s_Open) (latest at The Merit Club), the [2009 Solheim Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Solheim_Cup) (at Rich Harvest Farms), and the [2012 Ryder Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Ryder_Cup) (at Medinah). The [John Deere Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Deere_Classic) is a regular [PGA Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PGA_Tour) event played in the Quad Cities since 1971, whereas the [Encompass Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Encompass_Championship) is a [Champions Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Champions_Tour) event since 2013. Previously, the [LPGA State Farm Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LPGA_State_Farm_Classic) was an [LPGA Tour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LPGA_Tour) event from 1976 to 2011.&#x20;

## Law and politics

Main articles: [Law of Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_of_Illinois) and [Politics of Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Illinois)

In a 2020 study, Illinois was ranked as the 4th easiest state for citizens to vote in.[^cite_note-275]&#x20;

### State government

Main article: [Government of Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Illinois)



[JB Pritzker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JB_Pritzker) (D) \
[Governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_Illinois)



[Juliana Stratton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juliana_Stratton) (D) \
[Lieutenant Governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lieutenant_Governor_of_Illinois)



The [Illinois State Capitol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_State_Capitol) in [Springfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Springfield,_Illinois)

The [government of Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Illinois), under the [Constitution of Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Illinois), has three branches of government: executive, legislative, and judicial. The executive branch is split into several statewide elected offices, with the governor as chief executive. Legislative functions are granted to the Illinois General Assembly. The judiciary is composed of the Supreme Court and lower courts.&#x20;

The executive branch is composed of six elected officers and their offices as well as numerous other departments.[^cite_note-Lind2012ch5-276] The six elected officers are:[^cite_note-Lind2012ch5-276] [Governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_Illinois), [Lieutenant Governor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lieutenant_Governor_of_Illinois), [Attorney General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Attorney_General), [Secretary of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Secretary_of_State), [Comptroller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Comptroller), and [Treasurer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Treasurer). The government of Illinois has numerous departments, agencies, boards and commissions, but the so-called code departments provide most of the state's services.[^cite_note-Lind2012ch5-276][^cite_note-277] Since 2019, the Governor of Illinois is [JB Pritzker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JB_Pritzker) of the [Democratic Party](5043544.md).&#x20;



[Illinois House of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_House_of_Representatives)

The [Illinois General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_General_Assembly) is the state legislature, composed of the 118-member [Illinois House of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_House_of_Representatives) and the 59-member [Illinois Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Senate). The members of the General Assembly are elected at the beginning of each even-numbered year. The *[Illinois Compiled Statutes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Compiled_Statutes)* (ILCS) are the [codified](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Codification_\(law\)) statutes of a general and permanent nature.[^cite_note-survey-278][^cite_note-tapping-279]&#x20;

The [Judiciary of Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judiciary_of_Illinois) is the unified court system of Illinois. It consists of the [Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Illinois), [Appellate Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Appellate_Court), and [Circuit Courts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Circuit_Courts). The Supreme Court oversees the administration of the court system.&#x20;

The [administrative divisions of Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_Illinois) are counties, townships, precincts, cities, towns, villages, and special-purpose districts.[^cite_note-280] The basic subdivision of Illinois are the 102 counties.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECensus200789-281] Eighty-five of the 102 counties are in turn divided into townships and precincts.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECensus200789-281][^cite_note-282] Municipal governments are the cities, villages, and incorporated towns.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECensus200789-281] Some localities possess [home rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Home_rule), which allows them to govern themselves to a certain extent.[^cite_note-283]&#x20;

### Federal representation

Since 2023, Illinois is divided into 17 [congressional districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois%27s_congressional_districts). Each district sends one member to represent them in the [U.S. House of Representatives](19468510.md). Since congressional districts are based on population, the majority of districts are located in the [Chicago metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_metropolitan_area). Since the [2024 U.S. House elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_States_House_of_Representatives_elections), Illinois is represented by 14 [Democrats](5043544.md) and 3 [Republicans](32070.md). Like all other states, Illinois has two senators in the [U.S. Senate](24909346.md). The state's [senior senator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senior_senator) is [Dick Durbin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dick_Durbin) of [Springfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Springfield,_Illinois), who is also the [Senate Minority Whip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party_leaders_of_the_United_States_Senate), and its [junior senator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_senator) is [Tammy Duckworth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tammy_Duckworth) of [Hoffman Estates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoffman_Estates,_Illinois). Both Durbin and Duckworth are members of the [Democratic Party](5043544.md). Since the [2024 U.S. presidential election](59568546.md), Illinois sends 19 electors to the [Electoral College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Electoral_College).[^cite_note-284][^cite_note-285]&#x20;

In addition to Durbin, other Illinois federal politicians have served in high-ranking congressional positions. [Dennis Hastert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dennis_Hastert) (R) served as [Speaker of the U.S. House of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speaker_of_the_United_States_House_of_Representatives) from 1999 to 2007. [Rahm Emanuel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rahm_Emanuel) (D) served as Chair of the [House Democratic Caucus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_Democratic_Caucus) from 2007 to 2009 under [Nancy Pelosi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nancy_Pelosi). [Everett McKinley Dirksen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Everett_Dirksen) (R) served as [Senate Minority Leader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party_leaders_of_the_United_States_Senate) from 1959 to 1969. [Dan Rostenkowski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Rostenkowski) (D) served as Chair of the [House Ways and Means Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_House_Committee_on_Ways_and_Means) from 1981 to 1994.[^cite_note-286]&#x20;

### Party balance

In modern national and state politics, Illinois is a [Democratic stronghold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_states_and_blue_states).[^cite_note-287] Historically, Illinois was a political [swing state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swing_state), with near-parity existing between the [Republican](32070.md) and the [Democratic](5043544.md) parties. However, in recent elections, the Democratic Party has gained ground, and Illinois has come to be seen as a solid "blue" state in both presidential and congressional campaigns.[^cite_note-288][^cite_note-289] Illinois's Democratic tendencies are mostly attributable to [Cook County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cook_County,_Illinois) and [Chicago](6886.md), by far the state's largest county and city, respectively, which have long been strongly Democratic. The [collar counties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collar_counties), affluent suburban counties that surround Cook County, historically voted Republican and helped keep the state competitive; however, they have swung toward the left in recent elections as the national Republican Party has become increasingly conservative, which has cemented Democratic dominance in state politics.[^cite_note-290] [Outside of the Chicago metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downstate_Illinois), the state's rural areas are heavily Republican. Other downstate counties such as [Peoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peoria_County,_Illinois), [Champaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Champaign_County,_Illinois), [McLean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLean_County,_Illinois) ([Bloomington/Normal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bloomington%E2%80%93Normal)), [Rock Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_Island_County,_Illinois) ([Quad Cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quad_Cities)), and [St. Clair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Clair_County,_Illinois) ([East St. Louis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_St._Louis,_Illinois)) have trended Democratic in recent presidential and gubernatorial elections, each at their own pace, unlike most their neighbors. The dominance of the Chicago area in state elections is so overwhelming that it has influenced a [secessionist movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Separation_referendums_in_Illinois) in the downstate region.[^cite_note-291]&#x20;

Illinois was long seen as a national bellwether,[^cite_note-292] supporting the winner in every election in the 20th century, except for [1916](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1916_United_States_presidential_election_in_Illinois) and [1976](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_United_States_presidential_election_in_Illinois). Since the [1992 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1992_United_States_presidential_election), however, Illinois has trended more toward the Democratic Party and is part of the "[blue wall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_wall_\(U.S._politics\))" of states that have consistently voted Democratic in the last six presidential elections. In [2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_United_States_presidential_election_in_Illinois), [George W. Bush](3414021.md) became the first Republican to win the presidency without carrying either Illinois or [Vermont](32578.md), with [Donald Trump](4848272.md) repeating the feat in 2016 and 2024. Illinois has not elected a Republican to the [U.S. Senate](24909346.md) since [Mark Kirk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Kirk) won in [2010](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_United_States_Senate_elections_in_Illinois); the last Republicans to hold statewide office were Governor [Bruce Rauner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruce_Rauner) and Lieutenant Governor [Evelyn Sanguinetti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evelyn_Sanguinetti), who both left office in 2019. The [Democratic Party of Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Party_of_Illinois) has [supermajorities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supermajorities) in both chambers of the [Illinois General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_General_Assembly).[^cite_note-293] Of the state's 17 [congressional districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois%27s_congressional_districts), 14 are represented by Democrats.[^cite_note-294] The county boards of the state's five most populous counties ([Cook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cook_County,_Illinois), [DuPage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DuPage_County,_Illinois), [Lake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_County,_Illinois), [Will](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Will_County,_Illinois), and [Kane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kane_County,_Illinois)) have Democratic majorities along with some of the more populous downstate counties such as [St. Clair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Clair_County,_Illinois), [Champaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Champaign_County,_Illinois), [Peoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peoria_County,_Illinois), [McLean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLean_County,_Illinois), and [Rock Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_Island_County,_Illinois).[^cite_note-295]&#x20;

### History of corruption

Main article: [Political corruption in Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_corruption_in_Illinois)

Politics in the state have been infamous for highly visible corruption cases, as well as for crusading reformers, such as governors [Adlai Stevenson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adlai_Stevenson_II) and [James R. Thompson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_R._Thompson). In 2006, former governor [George Ryan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Ryan) was convicted of racketeering and bribery, leading to a six-and-a-half-year prison sentence. On December 7, 2011, former governor [Rod Blagojevich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rod_Blagojevich) was sentenced to 14 years in prison for allegations that he conspired to sell the vacated Senate seat left by President [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) to the highest bidder. Blagojevich had earlier been impeached and convicted by the legislature, resulting in his removal from office. In the late 20th century, Congressman [Dan Rostenkowski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Rostenkowski) was imprisoned for mail fraud; former governor and federal judge [Otto Kerner Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Otto_Kerner_Jr.) was imprisoned for bribery; Secretary of State [Paul Powell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Powell_\(politician\)) was investigated and found to have gained great wealth through bribes, and State Auditor of Public Accounts (Comptroller) [Orville Hodge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orville_Hodge) was imprisoned for embezzlement. In 1912, William Lorimer, the GOP boss of Chicago, was expelled from the U.S. Senate for bribery, and in 1921, Governor [Len Small](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Len_Small) was found to have defrauded the state of a million dollars. [Michael Madigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Madigan), Illinois' House Speaker for all but two years from 1983 to 2021, was sentenced to seven years in prison in 2025. Having served as chair of the [Democratic Party of Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Party_of_Illinois) concurrent with his speakership since 1997, Madigan was arguably the most powerful politician in Illinois.[^cite_note-Biles-30][^cite_note-Horsley-69][^cite_note-296]&#x20;

### U.S. presidential elections

Main article: [United States presidential elections in Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_presidential_elections_in_Illinois)



[2024 U.S presidential election](59568546.md) results by county in Illinois

Illinois has shown a strong presence in presidential elections. Three presidents have claimed Illinois as their political base when running for president: [Abraham Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham_Lincoln), [Ulysses S. Grant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ulysses_S._Grant), and most recently [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama). Lincoln was born in [Kentucky](16846.md), but he moved to Illinois at age 21. He served in the [General Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_General_Assembly) and represented the [7th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois%27s_7th_congressional_district) in the [U.S. House of Representatives](19468510.md) before his election to the presidency in 1860. Ulysses S. Grant was born in [Ohio](22199.md) and had a military career that precluded settling down, but on the eve of the [Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Civil_War) and approaching middle age, he moved to Illinois and thus utilized the state as his home and political base when running for president. Barack Obama was born in [Hawaii](13270.md) and made Illinois his home after graduating from [law school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_school), and later represented Illinois in the [U.S. Senate](24909346.md). He was then elected president in 2008, running as a candidate from his Illinois base.&#x20;

[Ronald Reagan](25433.md) was born in Illinois, in the city of [Tampico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tampico,_Illinois), raised in [Dixon, Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dixon,_Illinois), and educated at [Eureka College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eureka_College), outside [Peoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peoria,_Illinois). Reagan later moved to [California](5407.md) during his young adulthood. He then became an actor, and later became [California's Governor](62517.md) before being elected president.&#x20;

[Hillary Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillary_Clinton) was born and raised in the suburbs of Chicago and became the first woman to represent a major political party in the general election of the U.S. presidency. Clinton ran from a platform based in [New York State](8210131.md).&#x20;

### African-American U.S. senators

[Twelve African-Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_African-American_United_States_Senators) have served as members of the [United States Senate](24909346.md). Of which three have represented Illinois, the most of any single state: [Carol Moseley-Braun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carol_Moseley_Braun),[^cite_note-auto11-140] [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama),[^cite_note-297] and [Roland Burris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roland_Burris), who was appointed to replace Obama after his election to the presidency. Moseley-Braun was the first African-American woman to become a U.S. Senator.&#x20;

### Political families

Several families from Illinois have played particularly prominent roles in politics, in both the [Republican Party](32070.md) earlier in the state's history but more recently the [Democratic Party](5043544.md), gaining both statewide and national fame.&#x20;

#### Ingersoll

The [Ingersoll family of Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_political_families_\(I\)#The_Ingersolls_of_Illinois) comprised a pair of brothers who held several prominent elected positions representing Illinois.&#x20;

- [Ebon C. Ingersoll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ebon_C._Ingersoll) (1831–1879), Illinois State Representative 1856, U.S. Representative from Illinois 1864–71. Brother of Robert G. Ingersoll.

  [^cite_note-auto4-298]

- [Robert G. Ingersoll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_G._Ingersoll) (1833–1899), Illinois State Representative 1860, Attorney General of Illinois 1867–69, delegate to the Republican National Convention 1876. Brother of Ebon C. Ingersoll.

  [^cite_note-auto4-298]

  - John C. Ingersoll (1860–1903), U.S. Consul in [Cartagena, Colombia](63927.md) 1902. Son of Ebon C. Ingersoll.

    [^cite_note-auto4-298]

#### Stevenson

The [Stevenson family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stevenson_family), initially rooted in central Illinois and later based in the Chicago metropolitan area, has provided four generations of Illinois officeholders.&#x20;

- [Adlai Stevenson I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adlai_Stevenson_I) (1835–1914) was a Vice President of the United States, as well as a [Congressman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congressman)

- [Lewis Stevenson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lewis_Stevenson_\(politician\)) (1868–1929), son of Adlai, served as [Illinois Secretary of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Secretary_of_State).

- [Adlai Stevenson II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adlai_Stevenson_II) (1900–1965), son of Lewis, served as [Governor of Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_Illinois) and as the U.S. Ambassador to the United Nations; he was also the Democratic party's presidential nominee in [1952](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1952_United_States_presidential_election) and [1956](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1956_United_States_presidential_election), losing both elections to [Dwight Eisenhower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dwight_Eisenhower).

- [Adlai Stevenson III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adlai_Stevenson_III) (1930–2021), son of Adlai II, served ten years as a [United States Senator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Senator).

#### Daley

The [Daley family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daley_family)'s powerbase was in Chicago.&#x20;

- [Richard J. Daley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_J._Daley) (1902–1976) served as [Mayor of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor_of_Chicago) from 1955 to his death. He also served [Cook County State's Attorney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cook_County_State%27s_Attorney) from 1980 to 1989 and in the [Illinois Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Senate) from 1973 to 1980/

- [Richard M. Daley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_M._Daley) (born 1942), son of Richard J, was Chicago's longest-serving mayor, in office from 1989 to 2011.

- [William M. Daley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_M._Daley) (born 1948), another son of Richard J, is a former [White House Chief of Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_House_Chief_of_Staff) under President [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) and 32nd [United States Secretary of Commerce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Secretary_of_Commerce) under President [Bill Clinton](3356.md).

- [John P. Daley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_P._Daley) (born 1946), second-youngest child of Richard J, is a member of the [Cook County Board of Commissioners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cook_County_Board_of_Commissioners) since 1992, representing the 11th district.

- [Patrick Daley Thompson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_Daley_Thompson) (born 1969), grandson of Richard J and nephew of Richard M, served on the [Chicago City Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_City_Council) representing the 11th ward from 2015 until his resignation in 2022.

#### Pritzker

The [Pritzker family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pritzker_family) is based in Chicago and have played important roles in both the private and the public sectors.&#x20;

- [Jay Pritzker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jay_Pritzker) (1922–1999), co-founder of [Hyatt Hotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyatt_Hotel) based in Chicago.

- [Penny Pritzker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penny_Pritzker) (born 1959), 38th [United States Secretary of Commerce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Secretary_of_Commerce) under President [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama).

- [J. B. Pritzker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._B._Pritzker) (born 1965), current and 43rd [governor of Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_Illinois) and co-founder of the Pritzker Group.

#### Madigan

Members of the [Madigan family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Madigan#The_Madigan_family_and_their_role_in_Illinois_government) have held extensive influence in Illinois politics.&#x20;

- [Michael Madigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Madigan) (born 1942), longtime speaker of the [Illinois House of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_House_of_Representatives) and notorious [political boss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_boss).

- [Lisa Madigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lisa_Madigan) (born 1966), adopted daughter of Michael Madigan, former [Illinois Attorney General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Attorney_General).

## Education

Further information: [History of education in Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_education_in_Chicago)

### Illinois State Board of Education

Main article: [Illinois State Board of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_State_Board_of_Education)

The Illinois State Board of Education (ISBE) is autonomous of the governor and the state legislature, and administers [public education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_education) in the state. Local municipalities and their respective [school districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/School_district) operate individual public schools, but the ISBE audits performance of public schools with the [Illinois School Report Card](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_School_Report_Card). The ISBE also makes recommendations to state leaders concerning education spending and policies.&#x20;

### Primary and secondary schools

See also: [List of school districts in Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_school_districts_in_Illinois) and [List of high schools in Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_high_schools_in_Illinois)

Education is compulsory for ages 7–17 in Illinois. Schools are commonly, but not exclusively, divided into three tiers of primary and secondary education: elementary school, [middle school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_school) or junior high school, and high school. District territories are often complex in structure. Many areas in the state are actually located in *two* school districts—one for high school and the other for elementary and middle schools. And such districts do not necessarily share boundaries. A given high school may have several elementary districts that feed into it, yet some of those feeder districts may themselves feed into multiple high school districts.&#x20;

### Colleges and universities

For a more comprehensive list, see [List of colleges and universities in Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_colleges_and_universities_in_Illinois).

&#x20;Using the criterion established by [the Carnegie Foundation for the Advancement of Teaching](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Carnegie_Foundation_for_the_Advancement_of_Teaching), there are eleven "National Universities" in the state.



Campus of the [University of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Chicago)



[Foellinger Auditorium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foellinger_Auditorium) at the [University of Illinois Urbana-Champaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Illinois_Urbana-Champaign)

As of 19 August 2010, six of these rank in the "first tier" among the top 500 National Universities in the nation, as determined by the *[U.S. News & World Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._News_%26_World_Report)* rankings: the [University of Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Chicago), [Northwestern University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwestern_University), the [University of Illinois Urbana-Champaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Illinois_Urbana-Champaign), [Loyola University Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loyola_University_Chicago), the [Illinois Institute of Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Institute_of_Technology), [DePaul University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DePaul_University), [University of Illinois Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Illinois_Chicago), [Illinois State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_State_University), [Southern Illinois University Carbondale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Illinois_University_Carbondale), and [Northern Illinois University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Illinois_University).[^cite_note-usnews-299] The University of Chicago is continuously ranked as one of the world's top ten universities on various independent university rankings, and its [Booth School of Business](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Booth_School_of_Business), along with Northwestern's [Kellogg School of Management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kellogg_School_of_Management) consistently rank within the top five graduate business schools in the country and top ten globally. The University of Illinois Urbana-Champaign is often ranked among the best engineering schools in the world and United States.&#x20;

Illinois also has more than twenty additional [accredited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Educational_accreditation) four-year universities, both public and private, and dozens of small [liberal arts colleges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberal_arts_college) across the state. Additionally, Illinois supports 49 public [community colleges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Community_college) in the [Illinois Community College System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Community_College_System).&#x20;

### School financing

Schools in Illinois are funded primarily by property taxes, based on state assessment of property values, rather than direct state contributions. Scholar Tracy Steffes has described Illinois public education as historically "inequitable", a system where one of "the wealthiest of states" is "the stingiest in its support for education". There have been several attempts to reform school funding in Illinois. The most notable attempt came in 1973 with the adoption of the Illinois Resource Equalizer Formula, a measure through which it was hoped funding could be collected and distributed to Illinois schools more equitably. However, opposition from affluent Illinois communities who objected to having to pay for the less well-off school districts (many of them Black majority communities, produced by redlining, white flight, and other "soft" segregation methods) resulted in the formula's abolition in the late 1980s.[^cite_note-300]&#x20;

### Public libraries

In Illinois there are municipal public libraries (including those operated by cities, towns, townships, and villages), and there are libraries operated by special-purpose districts called library districts. As of 2026 state law allows the formation of county libraries, but as of that time there are none operating in the state.[^cite_note-301]&#x20;

## Transportation

See also: [List of Illinois Routes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Illinois_Routes)

Because of its central location and its proximity to the [Rust Belt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rust_Belt) and [Grain Belt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grain_Belt), Illinois is a national crossroads for air, auto, rail, and truck traffic.&#x20;

### Airports

See also: [List of airports in Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_Illinois)



Concourse B inside [O'Hare International Airport](248449.md), one of the [world's busiest airports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_busiest_airports_by_passenger_traffic)

Chicago's [O'Hare International Airport](248449.md) (ORD) is one of the busiest airports in the world, measured both in terms of total flights and passengers. It handled nearly 84.9 million passengers in 2025. It operates over 1,000 daily flights to 191 cities in the United States and 67 international destinations. It is a major [hub](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airline_hub) for both [United Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Airlines) and [American Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Airlines), and is undergoing a major \$8.5 billion expansion project. United Airlines is headquartered in Chicago. Chicago's [Midway International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Midway_International_Airport) (MDW) the secondary airport in the Chicago metropolitan area and also ranks as one of the nation's busiest airports. Midway is a major hub for [Southwest Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southwest_Airlines). Both airports are owned and operated by the Chicago Department of Aviation.[^cite_note-302][^cite_note-303][^cite_note-304]&#x20;

[Chicago/Rockford International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Rockford_International_Airport) (RFD) is one of the fastest growing air-cargo hubs in the world and is the 15th-busiest cargo airport in the United States.[^cite_note-305]&#x20;

Other commercial airports in Illinois include [Quad Cities International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quad_Cities_International_Airport) (MLI) in Moline, [General Wayne A. Downing International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peoria_International_Airport) (PIA) in Peoria, [Central Illinois Regional Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Illinois_Regional_Airport) (BMI) in Bloomington, [MidAmerica St. Louis Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MidAmerica_St._Louis_Airport) (BLV) in Belleville, [University of Illinois Willard Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Illinois_Willard_Airport) (CMI) in Savoy (Champaign), and [Abraham Lincoln Capital Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham_Lincoln_Capital_Airport) (SPI) in Springfield. The state is home to over 107 public/private airports.[^cite_note-306]&#x20;

### Highways

Main articles: [List of Interstate Highways in Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Interstate_Highways_in_Illinois) and [List of U.S. Highways in Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._Highways_in_Illinois)



An Illinois welcome sign along [U.S. Route 67](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_67_in_Illinois) in [Rock Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_Island,_Illinois)

The Interstate Highways in Illinois are all segments of the [Interstate Highway System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_Highway_System) that are owned and maintained by the state.[^cite_note-307] Major U.S. Interstate highways crossing the state include: [Interstate 24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_24_in_Illinois) (I-24), [I-39](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_39_in_Illinois), [I-41](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_41), [I-55](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_55_in_Illinois), [I-57](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_57_in_Illinois), [I-64](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_64_in_Illinois), [I-70](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_70_in_Illinois), [I-72](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_72_in_Illinois), [I-74](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_74_in_Illinois), [I-80](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_80_in_Illinois), [I-88](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_88_\(Illinois\)), [I-90](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_90_in_Illinois), and [I-94](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_94_in_Illinois).&#x20;

Illinois has 13 primary (two-digit) interstates pass through it, more than any other state. Illinois also ranks third among the fifty states with the most interstate mileage, coming in after California and Texas, which are much bigger states in area.[^cite_note-308]&#x20;



Standard license plate introduced in 2017

The [Illinois Department of Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Department_of_Transportation) (IDOT) is responsible for maintaining the [U.S Highways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Numbered_Highways) in Illinois. The system in Illinois consists of 21 [primary highways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._Highways). Among the U.S. highways that pass through the state, the primary ones are: [US 6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_6_in_Illinois), [US 12](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_12_in_Illinois), [US 14](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_14_in_Illinois), [US 20](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_20_in_Illinois), [US 24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_24_in_Illinois), [US 30](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_30_in_Illinois), [US 34](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_34_in_Illinois), [US 36](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_36_in_Illinois), [US 40](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_40_in_Illinois), [US 41](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_41_in_Illinois), [US 45](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_45_in_Illinois), [US 50](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_50_in_Illinois), [US 51](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_51_in_Illinois), [US 52](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_52_in_Illinois), [US 54](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_54_in_Illinois), [US 60](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_60_in_Illinois), [US 62](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_62_in_Illinois), and [US 67](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_67_in_Illinois).&#x20;

The [Illinois State Toll Highway Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Tollway) (more commonly known as the Illinois Tollway), operates five [toll roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toll_road) in the northern part of the state, with a sixth under construction. [I-Pass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I-Pass) is the Tollway's primary electronic transponder toll collection system. [E-ZPass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E-ZPass) is also accepted. The [Chicago Skyway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Skyway) is the only toll road in the state not operated by the Illinois Tollway, but instead by the Skyway Concession Company.[^cite_note-309]&#x20;

### Buses

#### Public Transit



Hydrogen-powered [MTD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Champaign%E2%80%93Urbana_Mass_Transit_District) bus in [Champaign–Urbana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Champaign%E2%80%93Urbana)

Many urban areas in Illinois are served by public transit networks consisting of fixed bus route and [demand-response](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demand-responsive_transport) services. In the Chicago area, under the oversight of the [Northern Illinois Transit Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Illinois_Transit_Authority) (NITA), the [Chicago Transit Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Transit_Authority) (CTA) and [Pace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pace_Suburban_Bus) provide bus service in the City of Chicago, suburban Cook County, and the five collar counties of DuPage, Lake, Kane, Will, and McHenry. In June 2026, the RTA will be replaced by the Northern Illinois Transit Authority (NITA).&#x20;

Major downstate transit systems include [MTD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Champaign%E2%80%93Urbana_Mass_Transit_District) in Champaign–Urbana, [Connect Transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Connect_Transit) in Bloomington–Normal, [CityLink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Peoria_Mass_Transit_District) in Peoria, [RMTD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rockford_Mass_Transit_District) in Rockford, [MetroLINK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quad_Cities_MetroLINK) in Moline, [SMTD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sangamon_Mass_Transit_District) in Springfield, [DeKalb Public Transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DeKalb_Public_Transit) in DeKalb, [River Valley Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/River_Valley_Metro_Mass_Transit_District) in Kankakee, and [DPTS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decatur_Public_Transit_System) in Decatur. The Illinois Department of Transportation (IDOT) supports downstate transit agencies through the Downstate Operating Assistance Program (DOAP).[^cite_note-310]&#x20;

Rural areas of Illinois are served through demand-response services. Examples include [C-CARTS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Champaign_County_Area_Rural_Transit_System) in Champaign County, [CountyLink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Peoria_Mass_Transit_District) in Peoria County, [Connect GO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Connect_Transit) in McLean County, RIM Rural Transit in Rock Island County, and [SHOW BUS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Show_Bus) in Kankakee County.[^cite_note-311]&#x20;

#### Inter-City Services

Illinois sees numerous intercity bus services primarily connecting east and west. The [Chicago Bus Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Bus_Station) is the busiest intercity bus station in the state. The following carriers provide scheduled service: [Amtrak Thruway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak_Thruway), [Barons Bus Lines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barons_Bus_Lines), [Burlington Trailways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burlington_Trailways), [Flixbus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flixbus), [Greyhound Lines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greyhound_Lines), [Indian Trails](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Trails), Miller Transportation (Hoosier Ride), [Peoria Charter Coach Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peoria_Charter_Coach_Company), [Van Galder Bus Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Van_Galder_Bus_Company), and [Wisconsin Coach Lines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisconsin_Coach_Lines).&#x20;

### Railroads

See also: [List of Illinois railroads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Illinois_railroads)



Two [IDOT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Department_of_Transportation)-owned [Amtrak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak) locomotives at [Chicago Union Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Union_Station)



[Metra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metra) commuter train in Chicago

Illinois has an extensive passenger and freight rail network.&#x20;

#### Passenger rail

[Chicago Union Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Union_Station) is the busiest rail station in the state, the fourth-busiest in the United States, and is a national hub for [Amtrak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak). Long-distance services from the [Eastern](509486.md), [Southern](179553.md), and [Western](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_United_States) United States meet in Chicago. Amtrak operates the [Illinois Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Service) in partnership with the [Illinois Department of Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Department_of_Transportation) (IDOT) featuring the Chicago to Carbondale [*Illini*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illini_\(Amtrak\)) and [*Saluki*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saluki_\(Amtrak\)), the Chicago to Quincy [*Carl Sandburg* and *Illinois Zephyr*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Zephyr_and_Carl_Sandburg), and the Chicago to St. Louis *[Lincoln Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lincoln_Service)*. The *Lincoln Service* is one of the few rail services outside the Northeastern United States with top speeds of up to 110 mph (180 km/h). Some of the state's busiest stations include [Uptown Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uptown_Station) in Bloomington–Normal and [Illinois Terminal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Terminal) in Champaign–Urbana. Amtrak's [Michigan Services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michigan_Services) are based in Chicago. Amtrak's [*Hiawatha*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hiawatha_\(Amtrak_train\)) and *[Borealis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borealis_\(train\))* are operated as part of partnership between IDOT and the Wisconsin Department of Transportation.[^cite_note-312]&#x20;

[Metra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metra) is one of the largest suburban commuter rail systems in the United States. Under the oversight of the [Northern Illinois Transit Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Illinois_Transit_Authority) (NITA), it operates 11 commuter rail lines and 243 stations in Cook, DuPage, Lake, Kane, Will, and McHenry counties, with one [line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Pacific_North_Line) extending into [Kenosha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenosha,_Wisconsin), [Wisconsin](33127.md). The [South Shore Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Shore_Line) commuter service originates in [Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millennium_Station) and connects the state to [Northwest Indiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwest_Indiana).[^cite_note-313]&#x20;

&#x20;The [Illinois Department of Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Department_of_Transportation) (IDOT) has programmed several new passenger rail routes to serve the state. These include the Chicago-[Moline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moline,_Illinois) [route](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quad_Cities_\(train\)) (to be operated by [Amtrak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak)) and the Chicago-[Rockford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rockford,_Illinois) [route](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rockford_Intercity_Passenger_Rail) (to be operated by [Metra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metra)).[^cite_note-314][^cite_note-315][^cite_note-316][^cite_note-317] A route between Chicago and [Peoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peoria,_Illinois) was accepted into the [Federal Railroad Administration's (FRA)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Railroad_Administration) [Corridor Identification and Development Program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corridor_Identification_and_Development_Program) and is undergoing studies.[^cite_note-318][^cite_note-319][^cite_note-320]



[Union Pacific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Pacific_Railroad) freight train in [Elmhurst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elmhurst,_Illinois)

#### Freight rail

&#x20;All six [Class I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Railroad_classes) freight railroads meet in Chicago, making it the largest and most active rail hub in North America. About 25 percent of all freight trains and 50 percent of all intermodal trains in the United States passes through the [Chicago area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_metropolitan_area). As a result, there is heavy rail congestion in the Greater Chicago area and [Chicago Region Environmental and Transportation Efficiency Program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Region_Environmental_and_Transportation_Efficiency_Program) (CREATE) program was established to improve the efficiency of freight and passenger rail movements.[^cite_note-321][^cite_note-322]



[CTA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Transit_Authority) [Blue Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Line_\(CTA\)) train in Chicago

#### Rapid transit and light rail

The [Chicago Transit Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Transit_Authority) (CTA) operates the [Chicago "L"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_%22L%22) rapid transit system. With eight color-coded lines and 146 stations, it is the third-busiest rapid transit system in the United States. The [Red](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Line_\(CTA\)) and [Blue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Line_\(CTA\)) Lines offer 24-hour service, one of the few rapid transit lines to do so in the world. The Blue and [Orange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orange_Line_\(CTA\)) Lines provide direct rail service to O'Hare and Midway Airports respectively. The Red Line is currently undergoing a \$5 billion extension project.[^cite_note-323]&#x20;

The [St. Louis MetroLink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MetroLink_\(St._Louis\)) operates two light rail lines in the [Metro-East region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metro_East) of Illinois.[^cite_note-324]&#x20;

### Waterways

See also: [Category\:Illinois waterways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Illinois_waterways)



A boat on the South Branch of the [Chicago River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_River)

Illinois is bordered by 880 miles of rivers, with the [Mississippi River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mississippi_River) and [Illinois River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_River) being among the largest. The Mississippi River provides access to the [Gulf of Mexico](21076367.md). Other major rivers include the [Ohio River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ohio_River), the [Wabash River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wabash_River), the [Kankakee River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kankakee_River), the [Kaskaskia River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaskaskia_River), the [Rock River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_River_\(Mississippi_River_tributary\)), and the [Fox River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_River_\(Illinois_River_tributary\)).[^cite_note-325]&#x20;

The [Chicago River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_River) hosts many tourist and commuter boat routes such as the Chicago River Architecture Tours and the Chicago Water Taxi.[^cite_note-326]&#x20;

[Lake Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Michigan) gives Illinois access to the [Atlantic Ocean](698.md) by way of the [Saint Lawrence Seaway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Lawrence_Seaway). Major harbors on Lake Michigan include [Waukegan Harbor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waukegan_Harbor_Light) in Waukegan and [North Point Marina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Point_Marina) in Winthrop Harbor. North Point Marina is the largest marina on the [Great Lakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Lakes).[^cite_note-327]&#x20;

The [Illinois International Port District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Chicago) operates the Port of Chicago, which is located on Lake Michigan at the mouth of the [Calumet River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calumet_River). It is the start point of the [Illinois Waterway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_Waterway), which is series of rivers that allow for commercial shipping between the Great Lakes and the Mississippi River. The Illinois Waterway also consists of the [Chicago Sanitary and Ship Canal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Sanitary_and_Ship_Canal), which connects the [Chicago River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_River) with the [Des Plaines River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Des_Plaines_River) and replaced the [Illinois and Michigan Canal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illinois_and_Michigan_Canal).[^cite_note-328]&#x20;

[^cite_note-7]: &#x20;Illinois borders the state of [Wisconsin](33127.md) to its north, [Iowa](26810748.md) to its northwest, [Missouri](19571.md) to its southwest, [Kentucky](16846.md) to its south, [Indiana](21883857.md) to its east, and has a water border with [Michigan](18859.md) to the northeast in [Lake Michigan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Michigan). The village of [Kaskaskia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaskaskia,_Illinois) in [Randolph County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Randolph_County,_Illinois), the original capital of Illinois, is where late-19th century flooding led to shifting of the Mississippi River, leaving the village and part of the county west of the Mississippi.

Cahokia was so large—covering three to five square miles—that archaeologists have yet to probe many portions of it. Its centerpiece was an open fifty-acre Grand Plaza, surrounded by packed-clay pyramids. The size of thirty-five football fields, the Grand Plaza was at the time the biggest public space ever conceived and executed north of Mexico ... a flat public square 1,600-plus feet in length and 900-plus feet in width.
